### [`RFC4271-9.1.2.1-2`](#rfc4271-9.1.2.1-2)

Unresolvable routes SHALL be removed from the Loc-RIB and the routing table. (§9.1.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::checkRouteBestChange moved. Same-scope independent rejudgment against the complete RFC 4271 Sections 9.1.2 and 9.1.2.1: "Unresolvable routes SHALL be removed from the Loc-RIB and the routing table." Re-read every tagged occurrence, the current selection/subscriber/removal path, both unchanged promoted daemon carriers and their complete shared fixture, sysrib withdrawal generation and the real fib-kernel writer. TestRFC4271NextHopLossReselectsAndRestorationRecovers separately checks preferred installation, reachable fallback after one cover disappears, complete BGP Loc-RIB removal after both disappear, retention of both received paths and recovery without another UPDATE. Its retained positive semantic record omits only no-candidate Loc-RIB removal; its negative record suppresses replacement mirroring and fails the still-reachable alternative, not separately recovery. Both Linux daemon placements send real iBGP UPDATEs through sockets, verify the configured internal or forked selecting-RIB placement, and observe BGP best, the selected Loc-RIB contribution and actual ip route reads across all kernel tables at each lifecycle stage. After both covers disappear, an absent BGP best and zero Loc-RIB candidate rows are required before an exact zero kernel-route count can pass. Query failures never count as absence. The retained CI positive records independently omit only runtime withdrawal handling in fibKernel.processEvent; their recorded clean carriers pass all phases and the recorded mutants pass the best/Loc-RIB absence gates before failing the exact kernel zero-route check on the stale proto-250 subject route. Ownership remains tracked and real shutdown flushing is checked before the fixture-owned link and OS route are removed, so forced cleanup does not create the live absence proof. Each retained CI negative record rejects terminal connected reachability and fails the initially legitimate independent BGP control while preserving the initial explicitly-gateway-resolved subject; it does not separately discriminate post-loss preservation or restoration. The unchanged clean lifecycle assertions still observe that direct control after loss and after recovery. This repair changes comments in recomputeForwarding and wraps the unchanged resolution body in fibEntry to suppress only otherwise valid targetless unicast output. These carriers retain explicit gateways or devices; loss of their BGP winner still takes the unchanged winner-nil withdrawal path before fibEntry. The tagged units, shared fixture, six recorded discrimination claims and their producing bodies are unchanged by this repair. The existing code map and enforced verdict are retained; no new execution, packet-forwarding verification, prospective-recursion integration or broader topology coverage is claimed. Together the retained Go Loc-RIB record and independent Linux kernel records continue to enforce both removal destinations in this bounded IPv4 internal/forked topology.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2.2-1`](#rfc4271-9.1.2.2-1)

The criteria MUST be applied in the order specified. (§9.1.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Independent rejudgment 2026-11-15 of the unchanged requirement and all ten tagged functions after four received-identifier fixture topology additions and the whole-set RIB consumer topology repair. RFC 4271 Section 9.1.2.2 states: 'The criteria MUST be applied in the order specified.' The full criteria (a)-(g), including whole-set elimination and MED comparability only within the neighboring AS, were read. Section 9.1.2 first excludes an unresolvable NEXT_HOP. Each structured and production-JSON identifier test now supplies only an independent connected 10.0.0.1/32 route, with real prospective Loc-RIB resolution and cost zero. UPDATE bytes, two candidates, peer addresses and BGP Identifiers are unchanged. The positive pair makes step f choose the higher-address peer with the lower BGP Identifier; the negative pair ties identifiers and requires step g to choose the lower peer address. These tests exercise metadata extraction through actual received events and assert exact comparison steps and winners. The topology adds no admission bypass and leaves unknown-next-hop controls unchanged. The unchanged adjacent-criteria test conflicts each earlier criterion with the next in both argument orders. The unchanged whole-set MED candidate tests cover all six permutations, require C to lose to A at MED before B beats A at Router ID, and forbid an earlier-criteria loser from eliminating C at MED. FullTiebreak and same-neighbor MED remain supplementary; the latter also prevents a different neighboring AS from winning through its lower MED. The selection producer removes earlier-criteria losers before grouping same-neighbor MED survivors and comparing later criteria. TestRFC4271WholeSetMEDRIBBestChange now supplies only independent connected routes to its unchanged next hops 192.0.2.3, 192.0.2.2 and 192.0.2.1. Its six permutations still exercise actual received attributes, exact Loc-RIB B-to-C-to-B selection as non-best MED witness A leaves and returns, exact replacement events and no spurious unchanged publication. Main's b6e1a9ef baseline passed this consumer with race (1.650s); the earlier 4d7e8db1 selected repaired RIB roots and peer regression passed with race and no skips. I inspected all five new RFC4271 normal-return semantic proof logs and their current native records. In d5b651a5 and 28d0bd7d, reversing compareAfterMED's Router ID comparison first passed the named structured or JSON baseline, then failed its exact winner assertion: expected 192.0.2.2, actual 192.0.2.1. In 147c6181 and c7ad240b, reversing the peer-address comparison first passed each equal-Identifier baseline, then failed its exact winner assertion: expected 192.0.2.1, actual 192.0.2.2. In 3c3ec480, replacing retainLowestMED's selected survivor count with the full eligible count first passed the whole-set RIB consumer baseline, then failed the first Loc-RIB winner assertion: expected B through 192.0.2.2, actual A through 192.0.2.1. The observed winner was A, not the proposal's predicted C; the actual incorrect winner is the evidence. These five native mutant records bind the changed unit bodies and each precise claim. They show ordinary assertion failures, not panics, skips or build failures, and do not extend proof to other breaks. Other tagged units, requirement quotes, levels and discrimination entries are unchanged. This pending file does not itself stamp or publish an audit.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2.2-3`](#rfc4271-9.1.2.2-3)

For IBGP- learned routes, the MULTI_EXIT_DISC MUST be used in route comparisons that reach this step in the Decision Process. (§9.1.2.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c13 judge). The MISSING clause (b) of Section 9.1.2.2 (c) is closed at the producer: firstASInPath answers 0 for a path that begins with an AS_SET (quote above the statement), and neighborAS turns 0 into the local AS for an IBGP route (PeerASN == LocalASN). Positive TestRFC4271IBGPAggregatesLedByASSetsCompareMED: two IBGP aggregates led by different AS_SETs share neighbor AS = local AS, MED 10 beats MED 20 at BestStepMED against IGP cost 50 vs 5, and SelectBest agrees. Negative TestRFC4271IBGPRouteLedByASSequenceKeepsItsNeighborAS: an AS_SEQUENCE-led IBGP route keeps neighbor AS 65010 against an aggregate led by AS_SET {65010, 65020}, MED is skipped and the IGP cost decides, so the local-AS rule is not over-applied. Both build candidates from wire bytes through firstASInPath and asPathLength, the calls extractCandidate (rib_commands.go) makes. Their records break the producer with a panic (reach only); a judge overlay (tree untouched) removing only the AS_SET check reddens both units and TestFirstASInPath, whose wrong expectation (65003, the set's first member) was corrected to 0 under D-15, so the clause is discriminated. HEAD units unchanged: empty-AS_PATH IBGP positive and the later-steps negative (2026-09-30 judge mutants). Side effect read: an EBGP route led by an AS_SET now compares no MED (route-selection.md); such a path breaks Section 5.1.2 b) at its sender, and MED is owed only between routes of one known neighbor AS, so not comparing it stays conformant. Residual outside this row's RFC 4271 reading: the RIB classifies IBGP by PeerASN == LocalASN, not the reactor's RFC 7705-aware rule (journal helper-bypassed-by-an-open-coded-copy.md).

### [`RFC4271-9.2-3`](#rfc4271-9.2-3)

If a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out, the previously advertised route in that Adj-RIB-Out MUST be withdrawn from service by means of an UPDATE message (§9.1.3)

Records cover only part of this requirement: tested "previously advertised route"; gap "a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out"; live general and route-server relays now withdraw the exact previously advertised IPv4 prefix after a newer received generation is rejected by export policy; accepted-generation controls carry the changed MED without withdrawal. Their shared-chain mutation can stop at the first general-rail failure, so it is not a separate observed route-server mutant red. Ordinary selected-route dissemination and its export-denial lifecycle remain unimplemented at internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::publishBestChanges; the ordinary selected-route probes remain red under spec-bgp-best-route-advertised-to-peers, and refresh or stale replay has separate unresolved scope under spec-bgp-refresh-reapplies-export-policy; they do not verify the whole requirement.

Audit verdict: partial (the tests enforce only the declared tested scope; the whole requirement remains unmet), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 9.1.3 says: If a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out, the previously advertised route in that Adj-RIB-Out MUST be withdrawn from service by means of an UPDATE message (see 9.2). Tested is "previously advertised route"; Gap is "a route in Loc-RIB is excluded from a particular Adj-RIB-Out"; Producer is internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rib_bestchange.go::publishBestChanges. Both relay matrices first assert the actual announcement, then either an exact bare withdrawal after export rejection of the newer received generation, or an accepted announcement carrying MED 77 and no withdrawal. The positive's shared-chain mutation may fail on the first general rail before the native route-server arm runs, so its named-unit red does not establish an independent route-server mutant red. These are manually driven relay proofs, not evidence that an ordinary Loc-RIB selection invokes an advertiser. The ordinary selected-route probes remain red, and publishBestChanges emits selection events rather than supplying that missing advertiser. Refresh and stale reconciliation retain separate unresolved scope in their owning spec. No complete Phase 3 or complete RFC support is claimed.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-4`](#rfc4271-9.2-4)

If a BGP speaker receives overlapping routes, the Decision Process MUST consider both routes based on the configured acceptance policy. (§9.1.4)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), stale-unit: internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_notify.go::Reactor.notifyMessageReceiverFrom, internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_notify.go::Reactor.notifyOneMessage moved. Independently re-read the full RFC 4271 Section 9.1.4 obligation: "If a BGP speaker receives overlapping routes, the Decision Process MUST consider both routes based on the configured acceptance policy." The receive positive requires both accepted /8 and /24 in Loc-RIB and exactly two adds; the receive negative checks both arrival orders, no withdrawal, the earlier route still present and two Adj-RIB-In entries. Both preserve the original UPDATE bytes and use the repaired real connected next-hop prerequisites. The unchanged reactor pair supplies the acceptance-policy boundary: registered ASN4 WireUpdate context permits the real policy-chain prefix read; the positive asks the chain twice and dispatches both NLRI in order, while the negative rejects the /24 and dispatches exactly the accepted /8. Its answer transport supplies the filter verdict rather than a parsed filter configuration, as in the existing claim. All four tagged carriers, the existing enforced verdict, MUST level and quoted requirement scope are retained. Main native receive refresh 32c5b989 recorded both existing receive covers with a named clean baseline PASS followed by intentional producer-disable panic red through checkRouteBestChange. Main native policy refresh 744678ce recorded both existing reactor covers with named clean baseline PASS followed by intentional producer-disable panic red: negative Reactor.runIngressPolicyChainMeta producer SHA 1434aa47d5cd0c08 and positive Reactor.notifyMessageReceiver SHA e77b6eb3fba4d9d7. Each retains its original unit, polarity, revert route and producer; no record was added or removed. These are observed producer-disable checks, not normal-return semantic-mutant proof or upgraded credit. The two policy records name the frozen Atomic working-tree producer bodies inspected for this judgment; those source changes were not yet in HEAD. Record and audit publication must be coordinated with Atomic's producer commit so the published dependency matches those observed bodies. Independent moved-producer source rejudgment: reread RFC 4271 Section 9.1.4, all four tagged carriers and assertion-producing helpers, and the full current notification/ingress/publication path. The reactor policy pair still presents both overlapping NLRI to the real configured library-chain answer transport; accepting both yields both in arrival order and rejecting only the more-specific yields only the accepted less-specific. The separate received-route pair still observes both Loc-RIB installations, no displacement on either arrival order and no withdrawal. notifyMessageReceiver is now a delegate to notifyMessageReceiverFrom, which owns receipt-aware continuations and invokes notifyOneMessage; the latter still applies acceptance policy before caching or consumer publication. Exact configured-Peer input prevents address-only lookup from substituting a different configured peer; legacy direct test callers pass nil and retain their existing bounded semantics. Preserve all four entries, exact MUST quote and enforced verdict. Renew only the existing positive entry-point body-panic record at the moved From function after source freeze, retaining its producer-reachability limitation; no new normal-return mutant or live source-session conformance is inferred. Final same-boundary receipt reconciliation: independently read complete job-closure-rfc-renewal-9-799bf60e.log and the current From/notifyOneMessage producer boundary. The exact positive overlapping-policy carrier passed clean and the intentional From body-panic produced its named red; native recording accepted the original positive cover at the moved function. This supersedes the earlier pending renewal language and retains the producer-reachability limitation, without claiming a new normal-return semantic mutant or rerun of the other three covers. Both named producer functions are wholly in HEAD-equal reactor_notify.go at HEAD 751524b526 after foundation d518d29e1c, so their code fingerprints bind the actual published enforcing source. All four original test entries remain.

### [`RFC4271-9.2.2.2-2`](#rfc4271-9.2.2.2-2)

ORIGIN attribute: If at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value INCOMPLETE, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value INCOMPLETE. Otherwise, if at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value EGP, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value EGP. (§9.2.2.2)

Audit verdict: unimplemented (no code path enforces the requirement), fresh. Re-judged by an independent judge after the row's annotation moved from {not-applicable} to {gap}. RFC 4271 Section 9.2.2.2 reads 'ORIGIN attribute: If at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value INCOMPLETE, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value INCOMPLETE.' and 'Otherwise, if at least one route among routes that are aggregated has ORIGIN with the value EGP, then the aggregated route MUST have the ORIGIN attribute with the value EGP.' Ze is the BGP speaker this binds once it aggregates. Aggregation is optional in Section 9.2.2.1, but no owner decision declines it or places it out of scope, so under the rule that an annotation proving less than a gap is written only for a role or feature the owner named, the absent feature is a conformance gap and the earlier not-applicable verdict no longer stands. Ze forms no aggregate: internal/component/bgp/message/update_build_grouped.go::BuildGroupedUnicast packs independent prefixes that share one attribute set into one UPDATE, and packGroupedAttributes copies the ORIGIN the caller supplied; no code derives an aggregate's ORIGIN from contributing routes. A configured covering static prefix takes whatever ORIGIN the operator writes, related to no contributing route.

No test carries RFC4271-9.2.2.2-2, so there is no linked test.

### [`RFC4271-Security-1`](#rfc4271-security-1)

An implementation MUST support the TCP MD5 option [RFC2385]. (§E)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_test.go::TestMD5PeersForListener, internal/component/bgp/reactor/reactor_test.go::TestMD5PeersForListener#2 moved. Collateral source re-judgment 2026-10-02 after unrelated MD5 requirement tags were removed from TestRFC2385MatchingKeysCarryASignedSession's enclosing doc comment. RFC4271 AppendixE states: An implementation MUST support the TCP MD5 option [RFC2385]. Its Security Considerations also require per-peer activation support. Every currently tagged unit was reread: TestRFC2385ConfiguredKeyReachesBothSockets drives an operator password through parsePeerFromTree and NewSession and asserts the exact key and peer address in RealDialer and md5PeersForListener; TestMD5PeersForListener and its second polarity assert two keyed default-port peers, MD5IP override, exclusion of an unkeyed peer and differently bound listener, the separate keyed custom-port peer, and an empty unrelated port. TestRFC2385MatchingKeysCarryASignedSession exercises actual RealDialer/RealListenerFactory sockets with matching keys and requires256KiB received; TestRFC2385KeyOnOneEndOnlyCarriesNoSession requires timeout for an unsigned dial into the keyed listener and fails on a successful connection, discriminating a no-op option installer. Source trace reaches reactor.newListenerFactory, which installs the collected keys in RealListenerFactory, and both socket Control callbacks reach md5_linux.go::setTCPMD5Sig before bind/connect with errors propagated. Configuration-only tests are supplementary, not a substitute for the real sockets. This preserves the Linux support verdict and does not claim macOS MD5 support, exact wire digest composition from the old loopback unit, or any new native execution. Unsupported-kernel skips are not positive evidence; the separately reviewed configured runtime and stock kernel emitted configs now establish TCP_MD5SIG availability. Existing executable assertions and this row's tag claims are unchanged; Main must stamp fresh enclosing-unit hashes with mode rejudge, not treat changed doc comments as mere shifted-file reseals.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-6`](#rfc4271-9.2-6)

When a BGP speaker receives an UPDATE message from an internal peer, the receiving BGP speaker SHALL NOT re-distribute the routing information contained in that UPDATE message to other internal peers (unless the speaker acts as a BGP Route Reflector [RFC2796]). (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c15 judge). Both c14 gaps on the general rail closed: TestRFC4271ForwardRailNeverRedistributesInternalRoutesToInternalPeers forwards one route from an internal non-client source on forwardUpdateCore -> forwardUpdateSection to an internal non-client (sent nothing), an external peer (sent the route) and an internal route-reflector client DESTINATION (sent the route: the RR exception with a client destination). Absence is judged after the two controls of the same fan-out arrive plus a 500 ms window, not a bare sleep. Revert record on forwardUpdateSection; the author's overlay turning the !srcInfo.isRRClient && !facts.rrClient refusal into if false reddens the non-client assertion (c15-ov-sh). The route-server rail keeps TestRFC4271NoIBGPToIBGPRedistribution (absence after a fixed 50 ms sleep, no in-fan-out control; its partner TestRFC4271IBGPRedistributionAllowedForReflectorClient shows the same dispatch within 2 s) and TestRFC4271IBGPRedistributionAllowedForReflectorClient: a residual timing weakness on that rail only, and neither RS unit has a record.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-7`](#rfc4271-9.2-7)

All newly installed routes and all newly unfeasible routes for which there is no replacement route SHALL be advertised to its peers by means of an UPDATE message. (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 4271 Section 9.2: "All newly installed routes and all newly unfeasible routes for which there is no replacement route SHALL be advertised to its peers by means of an UPDATE message." Re-judged 2026-10-26 by an independent auditor after the tags moved from plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_bestchange_test.go (745b0890f8) to reactor/rfc4271_rs_replacement_test.go. Both carriers drive the real bgp-rs, bgp-rib and bgp-adj-rib-in over live TCP sessions and judge the client's replayed TCP history behind a causal fence taken once the client owes nothing. Positive TestRFC4271RouteServerLastSourceWithdrawalIsSent: the only source withdraws P; the client no longer holds P, saw exactly one withdrawal of P and exactly one announcement (no re-announcement), so the no-replacement clause is asserted at the wire, not as an internal event. Negative TestRFC4271RouteServerOwnerWithdrawalSendsReplacement: the owner of the held best withdraws while another source's route is retained; the client ends on the retained route's exact attributes with zero withdrawals of P, which proves both the newly installed replacement is advertised and the bare withdrawal is confined to the no-replacement case; the earlier newly-installed steps (B's route, then A's better route reaching C) are also asserted. Discrimination records verified: revert of relay_recovery.go::queueRecoveryWithdrawal reddens the positive; mutant owesWithdrawal 'entry == nil' -> true (owner withdrawal sent bare) reddens the negative. The earlier weak finding (an internal BestChangeWithdraw event, no UPDATE, no newly-installed carrier) is answered by the new units. Scope read: owesWithdrawal/oweAnnouncement (session_ownership.go) and recoveryOwedFor (recovery_owed.go) do not branch on RSClient, so the RS-client fixture exercises the general non-ADD-PATH path; when the RIB recovery owner is not attached to the source, the bare withdrawal is sent with a warning naming the attach fix (ownerReceives), a misconfiguration path no tag covers. Independent companion-fixture rejudgment against RFC 4271 Section 9.2 and both complete tagged live-session carriers: the no-survivor case still requires the client to lose the prefix through exactly one withdrawal and no spurious announcement; the survivor case still requires the retained source's exact attributes and zero bare withdrawals, following demonstrated newly-installed announcements. Both judge bounded TCP history after settled decisions and a causal subsequent wire fence. The only affecting shared fixture repair supplies separately owned terminal interior host routes and asserts actual next-hop resolution before received BGP routes are judged. It neither creates the subject BGP prefixes nor changes their announcements, withdrawals, selection conditions, wire observations or assertions. Existing two-entry scope and enforced verdict remain; native producer-break renewal is separate from this source judgment. Independent acceptance of the actual negative retarget: read the complete native job-closure-rfc-renewal-27-8c3adeeb.log, the current complete tagged unit and Session.recoveryOwedFor producer, and the resulting native record. The named clean unit passed in 0.15s. The normal-return broken run passed the useful B-first then better-A baselines and reported only the post-A-withdrawal failures at rfc4271_rs_replacement_test.go:75 and :76: the recipient lost 203.0.113.0/24 instead of retaining B's exact attributes, and its withdrawal history was 1 rather than 0. There was no earlier baseline, setup, compiler or panic failure. Native recording accepted the existing negative cover with the new Session.recoveryOwedFor producer. This deliberately makes a registered, source-attached recovery owner appear unavailable and bypasses contested announcement election as well as owner-withdrawal election; it is an explicit broader same-claim semantic retarget, not a mechanical renewal of the historical narrower owesWithdrawal mutation. The actual assertions isolate the later replacement obligation despite that broader producer cut. The test body, tag claim, full two-entry row breadth and enforced verdict remain unchanged. Add the accepted stable Session.recoveryOwedFor producer as a code fingerprint boundary; native audit-stamp computes its value. This observation does not prove unready adjOutSelected behavior and does not stand in for the row's separate positive record renewal.

The wire carrier requires A's exact path, one announcement and zero withdrawals after settled decision and TCP fences; the Loc-RIB carrier waits until bgp-rib stores B's post-withdrawal marker before requiring A's still-feasible path and exact next hop in Loc-RIB. These are distinct wire and Loc-RIB observations confining removal to infeasible routes. This fixture maintenance changes sibling test setup in rfc4271_rib_bestchange_test.go while the tagged TestLocRIBMirror body and claims remain byte-identical. Main focused streaming baseline b535c0e1 passed that carrier within its ten-root scope, retaining its exact selected-path and post-withdrawal absence checks. Both route-server reactor negative carriers remain in the full three-cover population, and both existing normal-return MUTANT records remain unchanged. This maintenance claims no fresh execution of those reactor carriers and invents no positive discrimination receipt for TestLocRIBMirror; the enforced verdict and all existing limits remain unchanged. Independent companion-fixture rejudgment against RFC 4271 Section 9.2, all three tagged entries and their assertion-producing helpers: TestLocRIBMirror still installs the sole received BGP route, observes its selected path, removes its only Adj-RIB-In candidate and requires Loc-RIB absence. Both distinct negative reactor carriers still retain A's feasible route after B's worse route is announced and withdrawn, observing respectively exact settled client TCP history and Loc-RIB next hop after the source-storage marker barrier. The shared recovery fixture now installs independent terminal interior host routes and asserts next-hop resolution, without inserting any subject BGP prefix or changing the feasibility inputs, withdrawal, marker, retained path or absence assertions. ### [`RFC4271-9.2-9`](#rfc4271-9.2-9)

Changes to the reachable destinations within its own autonomous system SHALL also be advertised in an UPDATE message. (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Re-judged 2026-10-01 (BGP c18 judge). The old objection (units call the encoders only) is closed at the entry point: NEW TestRFC4271OwnASDestinationBecomingReachableIsSentToPeers originates 10.20.0.0/24 through reactorAPIAdapter.AnnounceNLRIBatch (the API announce entry point) toward one internal and one external Established peer and parses each connection's bytes: exactly one UPDATE, Withdrawn Routes empty, attributes present, NLRI = the prefix. NEW TestRFC4271OwnASDestinationBecomingUnreachableIsSentToPeers: after that announcement WithdrawNLRIBatch writes exactly one more UPDATE per peer with the prefix in Withdrawn Routes, no attributes, no NLRI, so a change that removes reachability is advertised, not left standing. Observed-red records: + on reactor_api_batch.go::AnnounceNLRIBatch, - on WithdrawNLRIBatch. The HEAD encoder units (writeAnnounceUpdate/writeWithdrawUpdate) stay as supplementary. Residual, not a clause gap: own-AS reachability changes are driven through the API rail; static-route reload and default-originate rails are not exercised by these units.

### [`RFC4271-9.2-10`](#rfc4271-9.2-10)

If, due to the limits on the maximum size of an UPDATE message (see Section 4), a single route doesn't fit into the message, the BGP speaker MUST not advertise the route to its peers and MAY choose to log an error locally. (§9.2)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/bgp/message/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271FittingRouteIsAdvertised, internal/component/bgp/message/rfc4271_test.go::TestRFC4271OversizeSingleRouteNotAdvertised moved. RFC 4271 Section 9.2: “If, due to the limits on the maximum size of an UPDATE message (see Section 4), a single route doesn't fit into the message, the BGP speaker MUST not advertise the route to its peers and MAY choose to log an error locally.” The changed splitter pair retains equivalent non-ADD-PATH framing. Its oversized opaque-attribute case requires an error and zero callbacks; the fitting control preserves attributes and NLRI in one output. The genuine relay pair supplies protocol-shaped input: a single route with 900 communities fits and is relayed byte-identically; 1100 communities exceed the 4096-byte destination limit and yield ok=false with neither raw nor parsed output. Both forwarding callers explicitly release and skip failed items, so failure is not later dispatched. Final ordinary-write admission also refuses an indivisible effective chunk. The corrected test tag accurately says an error, not an assertion of the exact ErrAttributesTooLarge identity. Logging is optional. Whole-producer halt records are reachability evidence, not selective size-check mutations.

### [`RFC4271-9.1.2-5`](#rfc4271-9.1.2-5)

If the AS_PATH attribute of a BGP route contains an AS loop, the BGP route should be excluded from the Phase 2 decision function (§9.1.2). Detection scans the full AS path and checks that the local autonomous system number does not appear in it. RFC 4271 writes this keyword in lower case, so the level is a recommendation and not a capitalized RFC 2119 SHOULD. The same paragraph places a speaker configured to accept routes with its own autonomous system number in the AS path outside the scope of the document. That out-of-scope case is what the allow-own-as setting selects, so a non-zero allow-own-as is not a deviation from this line.

### [`RFC4271-4.3-7`](#rfc4271-4.3-7)

A BGP speaker SHOULD treat an UPDATE message of this form as though the WITHDRAWN ROUTES do not contain the address prefix. (§4.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), shifted: internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_mixed_update_test.go::TestRIBInjectSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_mixed_update_test.go::TestRIBPoolPathSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute, internal/component/bgp/plugins/rib/rfc4271_rib_mixed_update_test.go::TestRIBSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute moved. Section 4.3 says a mixed UPDATE should be treated as though WITHDRAWN ROUTES did not contain the duplicated prefix. The three existing positives remain: structured receive, JSON/pool receive and injection keep that prefix in their Adj-RIB-In; the structured positive additionally requires the exact BestChangeAdd, no withdrawal, and the same outcome on a repeat UPDATE. Its setup-only repair adds a real connected 10.0.0.1/32 without changing either mixed wire message or assertions. The receive negative names 10/8 and 11/8 withdrawn but reannounces only 10/8, then requires 10/8 present and 11/8 absent from both views, excluding an implementation that ignores the entire withdrawal field. Independent rejudgment preserves the existing verdict, requirement quote, level and full tagged scope. Main receive baseline 4f70ab4b passed all 11 roots (12 leaf cases), race enabled, without skips; supplementary baseline d3723dd3 passed TestLocRIBMirror and TestRIBSamePrefixInWithdrawnAndNLRIInstallsTheRoute. Main native refresh 32c5b989 completed all 13 existing receive-cover records: each named clean baseline passed and its existing revert route disabled the named producer with an intentional panic, producing the recorded red. Those observations retain their original producer-disable route and provenance; they are not normal-return semantic-mutant proof and do not add credit to unrecorded supplementary carriers.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-5`](#rfc4271-6.3-5)

If any recognized attribute has Attribute Flags that conflict with the Attribute Type Code, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Attribute Flags Error. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 3(c) to treat-as-withdraw unless the attribute's specification mandates other flag handling. Flag conflicts are decided by one table (AttributeCode.FlagsConflict via validateAttributeFlags). Tagged units now cover ORIGIN (well-known), MED, AGGREGATOR and ATOMIC_AGGREGATE with conflicting Optional/Transitive bits: exact withdraw-only payload, session Established; the same attributes with specified flags keep the prefix and the value byte for byte. AGGREGATOR flags go to withdraw because RFC 7606 7.7 mandates attribute discard only for its length conditions, not for flags. Observed-red records on validateAttributeFlags both polarities.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-6`](#rfc4271-6.3-6)

If any recognized attribute has an Attribute Length that conflicts with the expected length (based on the attribute type code), then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Attribute Length Error. The Data field MUST contain the erroneous attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 to per-attribute outcomes. Every attribute whose length its type code fixes now has a length case against its expected length: ORIGIN (withdraw, 7.1), NEXT_HOP (withdraw, 7.3) and MED in TestSessionRFC4271RevisedAttributeErrors, ATOMIC_AGGREGATE and AGGREGATOR (attribute discard, prefix kept, 7.6/7.7) in TestRFC4271RecognizedAttributeErrorsBeyondTheFirst, and LOCAL_PREF in TestRFC4271LocalPrefLengthFromInternalPeer: an iBGP session over the real receive path, length 3 yields exactly the withdrawal of the prefix with the session still Established (7.5), length 4 before and after keeps the prefix with LOCAL_PREF byte-identical. The LOCAL_PREF buffer is otherwise valid, so only the length rule can fire. Observed-red records on validateLocalPrefAttr, validateAggregatorAttr and validateNextHopAttr, both polarities.

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 7606 Section 3(d) (Updates: 4271) replaces the subcode and Data clauses with treat-as-withdraw, so the forbidden behaviour is a reset or a silent keep. TestSessionRFC4271RevisedAttributeErrors: missing ORIGIN, missing AS_PATH and missing NEXT_HOP each require.Equal the payload to a withdrawal of 203.0.113.0/24 (red on a keep or a drop), and the good UPDATE after it on the same session is received (red on a reset). Negative: all three present, NLRI announced. TestRFC4271MandatoryAttributesAcrossUpdateForms pins the MP_REACH/legacy NEXT_HOP split.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-11`](#rfc4271-6.3-11)

The IP address in the NEXT_HOP MUST meet the following criteria to be considered semantically correct: a) It MUST NOT be the IP address of the receiving speaker. b) In the case of an EBGP, where the sender and receiver are one IP hop away from each other, either the IP address in the NEXT_HOP MUST be the sender's IP address that is used to establish the BGP connection, or the interface associated with the NEXT_HOP IP address MUST share a common subnet with the receiving BGP speaker. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. criterion a) receiver's own address (eBGP and iBGP) and b) off-link next hop on a one-hop eBGP session each withhold the legacy announcement on the live receive path; sender address and on-subnet third party accepted; multihop and iBGP accept off-link; interface snapshot change flips the verdict. The reaction (ignore, no NOTIFICATION) is RFC4271-6.3-2/6.3-3

### [`RFC4271-6.3-12`](#rfc4271-6.3-12)

If the path is syntactically incorrect, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Malformed AS_PATH. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 7606 (Updates: 4271) revises this: the error is handled by treat-as-withdraw, so no NOTIFICATION, subcode or Data field is sent. unknown segment type, zero segment count, segment overrun and trailing octet each withdraw the prefix exactly (7.2); a well-framed AS_SEQUENCE announces

### [`RFC4271-6.3-13`](#rfc4271-6.3-13)

If the UPDATE message is received from an external peer, the local system MAY check whether the leftmost (with respect to the position of octets in the protocol message) AS in the AS_PATH attribute is equal to the autonomous system number of the peer that sent the message. If the check determines this is not the case, the Error Subcode MUST be set to Malformed AS_PATH. (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. RFC 7606 (Updates: 4271) revises this: the error is handled by treat-as-withdraw, so no NOTIFICATION, subcode or Data field is sent. an external UPDATE whose leftmost AS 65003 is not the peer AS 65002 is delivered as exactly a withdrawal of its prefix (7.2); leftmost AS equal to the peer AS announces unchanged

### [`RFC4271-6.3-14`](#rfc4271-6.3-14)

If an optional attribute is recognized, then the value of this attribute MUST be checked. If an error is detected, the attribute MUST be discarded, and the Error Subcode MUST be set to Optional Attribute Error. The Data field MUST contain the attribute (type, length, and value). (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606. 'If an optional attribute is recognized, then the value MUST be checked': RFC 4271's own optional attributes, MED (3 octets -> exact withdrawal, 4 kept) and AGGREGATOR (bad length -> discarded, prefix kept, the 'attribute MUST be discarded' clause), are both covered, plus COMMUNITIES and EXTENDED COMMUNITIES (withdraw per 7606 7.8/7.14) with valid values kept byte for byte. Observed-red records on validateCommunityAttr both polarities. ORIGINATOR_ID and CLUSTER_LIST value checks belong to the RFC 4456 / RFC 7606 per-attribute rows, not this generic RFC 4271 row.

### [`RFC4271-6.3-15`](#rfc4271-6.3-15)

If any attribute appears more than once in the UPDATE message, then the Error Subcode MUST be set to Malformed Attribute List (§6.3)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Revised by RFC 7606 Section 3(g): a duplicate MP_REACH_NLRI or MP_UNREACH_NLRI draws NOTIFICATION Malformed Attribute List; any other duplicate is discarded after the first. TestRFC4271UpdateMalformedAttributeList: duplicate MP_REACH_NLRI -> NOTIFICATION 3/1 read off the socket; duplicate COMMUNITY -> no NOTIFICATION, exactly one COMMUNITY left (negative). 2026-10-01 re-judge (BGP c9 judge, wrote none of these units): the MP_UNREACH clause is now tagged: TestSessionRFC7606DuplicateMPUnreachNotificationOnTheWire reads 3/1 off the wire for a duplicate MP_UNREACH_NLRI, and its fixture (ORIGIN, empty AS_PATH, two well-formed MP_UNREACH, no NLRI) records no other error, so only the duplicate check produces the reset (RFC7606-3.g-1 mutant B evidence). Dropping the mpUnreachCount operand now turns a tagged unit red. ValidateUpdateRFC7606AddPath breaks observed red for all three units.

### [`RFC4271-9-4`](#rfc4271-9-4)

Otherwise, if the Adj-RIB-In has no route with NLRI identical to the new route, the new route SHALL be placed in the Adj-RIB-In. (§9)

Audit verdict: enforced (the tests do what the requirement demands), fresh. Forbidden: a new NLRI not placed. handleReceivedStructured (the reactor UPDATE entry) asserts Len 1 then 2 and Get(10.0.0.0/8) with its own NEXT_HOP, red on a route not stored. Negative (the condition false, identical NLRI): Len stays 2 and the slot holds the newer NEXT_HOP, red on a route placed beside the existing one.

## Superseded

No document obsoletes RFC 4271, so its obligations are stated where they were written.
